Remarks.&mdash;During July the highest reading of the barometer was 30.044, on the 9th; and the lowest 29.720, on the 21st. The highest temperature was 95&deg;, on the 13th and 14th; the lowest 73&deg;, on the 4th; and the mean during month, 83&deg;.7. S.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 16th, averaging 9.3 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 17 days, measuring 4.63 inches. During August the highest reading of the barometer was 30.094, on the 8th; and the lowest 29.755, on the 13th. The highest temperature was 96&deg;, on the 20th; the lowest 76&deg;, on the 22nd; and the mean during month, 85&deg;. S.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 20th, averaging 9.7 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 20 days, measuring 4.47 inches. During September the highest reading of the barometer was 30.1S0, on the 18th; and the lowest 29.430, on the 9th. The highest temperature was 970, on the 2nd; the lowest 69&deg;, on the 10th; and the mean during month, 82&deg;.6. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 8th, averaging 25.2 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 13 days, measuring 4.93 inches. IX. 1893.] NOTIFICATIONS. 165 No. 273. CANTON DISTRICT. MACAO FORT PASSAGE&mdash;CANTON RIVER. Bird&#39;s Nest Rocks Buoy. Notice is hereby given that a conical red buoy, feet in diameter, has been moored in 13 feet at low water spring tides off the south-western end of the Bird&#39;s Nest Rocks, in the Macao Fort Passage of the Canton River. Shanghai, 21 st August 1893. No. 274. F00CH0W DISTRICT. CHANNEL BETWEEN TURNABOUT AND HAITAN ISLANDS. The subjoined information has been received from Captain Charles J. Norcock, R.N., Commanding H.M.S. Caroline:&mdash; &quot;H.M.S. &#39;Caroline,&#39; &quot;At Shanghai, 28th August 1893. &quot; HYDROGRAPHICAL NOTE. &quot;Shoal Patch between Turnabout and Haitan Islands. &quot; Position:&mdash; &quot;Latitude 250 26&#39; 30&quot; N. Longitude 1190 53&#39; 45&quot; E. &quot; Personal Examination. &quot;This is a rocky patch, of 6 to 10 fathoms, lying midway between Haitan and Turnabout Islands, 5 cables long, in a N.E. and S.W. direction, and 3 cables broad. Least water found, 6 fathoms, rock. &quot; At low water there is a very heavy tide rip, and the water is much discoloured over the patch. As there may be similar patches between these islands, it is suggested that vessels pass outside Turnabout. &quot;(Signed) WM. PHILIP LODDER, &quot; Navigating Officer. &quot; Approved. &quot;(Signed) CHAS. J. NORCOCK, &quot; Captain.&quot; Shanghai, 29th August 1893.

The subjoined Notice to Mariners has been received from the Chief Commissioner of Customs, Corea:&mdash; &quot; COREA. &quot; NOTICE TO MARINERS, &quot;No. 5. &quot;JENCHUAN DISTRICT. &quot;SUNKEN ROCK IN LYNE SOUND, NEAR PORT OF MOKPO. &quot; A sunken rock, with 6 feet of water at low water spring tides, lies in latitude 34&deg; 36&#39; 15&quot; N., longitude 126&deg; 12&#39; 45&quot; E. The rock is of an oval shape, 150 yards long by 50 yards broad, lying S.E. and N.W. about 1 mile from a small conical island (name unknown) bearing W.S.W. from the rock. &quot; The channel in which this danger lies is not recommended as an approach to Mokpo. &quot; By Order of the Chief Commissioner of Customs, &quot;(Signed) W. McC. OSBORNE, &quot; Acting Commissioner of Customs. &quot;Custom House, &quot; Jenchuan, 31 si August 1893.&quot; Shanghai, i 8th September 1893.
